## Education





[Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) ([Widener Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Widener_Library) at top) and [MIT](18879.md) ([MIT Building 10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIT_Building_10) at bottom) are regarded in the world's top few universities for academic [research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research) in various disciplines.

[^cite_note-AcademicRanking2-68]

Further information: [Education in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Massachusetts) and [History of education in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_education_in_Massachusetts)



Towns in Massachusetts by combined mean SAT of their public high school district for the 2015–2016 academic year

[^cite_note-245]

In 2018, Massachusetts's overall educational system was ranked the top among all fifty U.S. states by *[U.S. News & World Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report)*.[^cite_note-246] Massachusetts was the first state in North America to require municipalities to appoint a teacher or establish a grammar school with the passage of the [Massachusetts Education Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_School_Laws) of 1647,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDejnozkaGiffordKapelKapel1982313-247] and 19th century reforms pushed by [Horace Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horace_Mann) laid much of the groundwork for contemporary universal public education[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDejnozkaGiffordKapelKapel1982311-248][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldfieldAbbottAndersonArgersinger1998251–52-249] which was established in 1852.[^cite_note-compschools-136] Massachusetts is home to the oldest school in continuous existence in North America ([The Roxbury Latin School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roxbury_Latin_School), founded in 1645), as well as the country's oldest public elementary school ([The Mather School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mather_School), founded in 1639),[^cite_note-250] its oldest high school ([Boston Latin School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Latin_School), founded in 1635),[^cite_note-251] its oldest continuously operating boarding school ([The Governor's Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Governor's_Academy), founded in 1763),[^cite_note-252] its oldest college ([Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University), founded in 1636),[^cite_note-253] and its oldest women's college ([Mount Holyoke College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Holyoke_College), founded in 1837).[^cite_note-254] Massachusetts is also home to the highest ranked private high school in the United States, [Phillips Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phillips_Academy) in [Andover, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andover,_Massachusetts), which was founded in 1778.[^cite_note-255]

Massachusetts's per-student public expenditure for elementary and secondary schools was eighth in the nation in 2012, at \$14,844.[^cite_note-256] In 2013, Massachusetts scored highest of all the states in math and third-highest in reading on the [National Assessment of Educational Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assessment_of_Educational_Progress).[^cite_note-257] The state's public school students are in the top tier of global academic performance.[^cite_note-AcademicRanking3-71] In 2022, Massachusetts was first or second in the reading and mathematics average scores and proficiency percentages of the evaluated 4th and 8th graders.[^cite_note-258]

Massachusetts hosts 121 institutions of higher education.[^cite_note-259] [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) and the [Massachusetts Institute of Technology](18879.md), both located in [Cambridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge,_Massachusetts), consistently rank among the world's best private universities and universities in general.[^cite_note-260] In addition to Harvard and MIT, several other Massachusetts universities rank in the top 50 at the undergraduate level nationally in the [widely cited rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_and_university_rankings#United_States) of *[U.S. News & World Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report)*: [Tufts University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tufts_University) (#27), [Boston College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_College) (#32), [Brandeis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandeis_University) (#34), [Boston University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_University) (#37) and [Northeastern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_University) (#40). Massachusetts is also home to three of the top five *U.S. News & World Report*'s best Liberal Arts Colleges: [Williams College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_College) (#1), [Amherst College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amherst_College) (#2), and [Wellesley College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellesley_College) (#4).[^cite_note-261] It is also home to the oldest Catholic liberal arts college, [College of the Holy Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_the_Holy_Cross) (#33).[^cite_note-262] [Boston Architectural College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Architectural_College) is New England's largest private college of [spatial design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spatial_design). The public [University of Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Massachusetts) (nicknamed *UMass*) features five campuses in the state, with its [flagship campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Massachusetts_Amherst) in [Amherst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amherst,_Massachusetts), which enrolls more than 25,000.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264]

As of 2021, Massachusetts has the [highest percentage of adults over the age of 25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Educational_attainment_in_the_United_States) with a bachelor's degree (46.62%) and a [graduate](153981.md) degree (21.27%) of any state in the country.

## Economy

See also: [List of Massachusetts locations by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Massachusetts_locations_by_per_capita_income) and [Category\:Economy of Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Economy_of_Massachusetts)

According to the United States [Bureau of Economic Analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis), Massachusetts's [gross state product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_state_product) was \$820.104 billion and its per capita personal income was \$97,456 in 2025.[^cite_note-265] As of January 2023, Massachusetts state general [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage) is \$15.00 per hour while the minimum wage for tipped workers is \$6.75 an hour, with a guarantee that employers will pay the difference should a tipped employee's hourly wage not meet or exceed the general minimum wage.[^cite_note-266] These minimum wage levels came about in January 2023, as part of a series of minimum wage amendments passed in 2018 that saw the minimum wage increase slowly every January up to 2023.[^cite_note-267] In 2025, 99.5% of businesses in the state were [small businesses](276495.md) and employed 1.5 million people, or 44.7% of the state's work force.[^cite_note-268]

In 2015, twelve [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies were located in Massachusetts: [Liberty Mutual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Mutual), [Massachusetts Mutual Life Insurance Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Mutual_Life_Insurance_Company), [TJX Companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TJX_Companies), [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric), [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon), [American Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Tower), [Global Partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Partners), [Thermo Fisher Scientific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermo_Fisher_Scientific), [State Street Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Street_Corporation), [Biogen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biogen), [Eversource Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eversource_Energy), and [Boston Scientific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Scientific).[^cite_note-269] CNBC's list of "Top States for Business for 2023" has recognized Massachusetts as the 15th-best state in the nation for business,[^cite_note-270] and for the second year in a row in 2016 the state was ranked by Bloomberg as the most innovative state in America.[^cite_note-271] According to a 2013 study by Phoenix Marketing International, Massachusetts had the sixth-largest number of millionaires per capita in the United States, with a ratio of 6.73 percent.[^cite_note-272] Billionaires living in the state include past and present leaders (and related family) of local companies such as [Fidelity Investments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidelity_Investments), [New Balance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Balance), [Kraft Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraft_Group), Boston Scientific, and the former [Continental Cablevision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Cablevision).[^cite_note-273]

Massachusetts has three [foreign-trade zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign-trade_zones_of_the_United_States), the [Massachusetts Port Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Port_Authority) of Boston, the Port of New Bedford, and the City of Holyoke.[^cite_note-274] [Boston-Logan International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_International_Airport) is the busiest airport in New England, serving 33.4 million total passengers in 2015, and witnessing rapid growth in international air traffic since 2010.[^cite_note-LoganTraffic-275]

Sectors vital to the Massachusetts economy include higher education, [biotechnology](4502.md), [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology), finance, health care, tourism, manufacturing, and defense. The [Route 128 corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Route_128_\(Massachusetts\)) and Greater Boston continue to be a major center for [venture capital investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capital),[^cite_note-276] and [high technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_technology) remains an important sector. In recent years tourism has played an ever-important role in the state's economy, with Boston and [Cape Cod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Cod) being the leading destinations.[^cite_note-277] Other popular tourist destinations include [Salem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem,_Massachusetts), [Plymouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth,_Massachusetts), and [the Berkshires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Berkshires). Massachusetts is the sixth-most popular tourist destination for foreign travelers.[^cite_note-278] In 2010, the Great Places in Massachusetts Commission published '1,000 Great Places in Massachusetts' that identified 1,000 sites across the commonwealth to highlight the diverse historic, cultural, and natural attractions.[^cite_note-279]



Sunset at [Brewster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brewster,_Massachusetts), on [Cape Cod Bay](1246817.md).

While manufacturing comprised less than 10% of Massachusetts's gross state product in 2016, the Commonwealth ranked 16th in the nation in total manufacturing output in the United States.[^cite_note-280] This includes a diverse array of manufactured goods such as medical devices, paper goods, specialty chemicals and plastics, telecommunications and electronics equipment, and machined components.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282]

The more than 33,000 nonprofits in Massachusetts employ one-sixth of the state's workforce.[^cite_note-283] In 2007, Governor Deval Patrick signed into law a state holiday, Nonprofit Awareness Day.[^cite_note-284]

In February 2017, *[U.S. News & World Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report)* ranked Massachusetts the best state in the United States based upon 60 [metrics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performance_metric) including healthcare, education, crime, infrastructure, opportunity, economy, and government. Massachusetts ranked number one in education, number two in healthcare, and number five in the handling of the economy.[^cite_note-best_states-285]

As of May 2025, the [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_in_the_United_States) in the state was 4.8%

### Taxation

Depending on how it is calculated, state and local tax burden in Massachusetts has been estimated among U.S. states and Washington D.C. as 21st-highest (11.44% or \$6,163 per year for a household with nationwide median income)[^cite_note-286] or 25th-highest overall with below-average corporate taxes (39th-highest), above-average personal income taxes, (13th-highest), above-average sales tax (18th-highest), and below-average property taxes (46th-highest).[^cite_note-287] In the 1970s, the Commonwealth ranked as a relatively high-tax state, gaining the pejorative nickname "Taxachusetts". This was followed by a round of tax limitations during the 1980s—a conservative period in American politics—including [Proposition 2½](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposition_2%C2%BD).[^cite_note-288]

As of January 1, 2020, Massachusetts has a flat-rate personal income tax of 5.00%,[^cite_note-289] after a 2002 voter referendum to eventually lower the rate to 5.0%[^cite_note-290] as amended by the legislature.[^cite_note-291] There is a [tax exemption](960403.md) for income below a threshold that varies from year to year. The corporate income tax rate is 8.8%,[^cite_note-MassTaxes1-292] and the short-term [capital gains tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_gains_tax) rate is 12%.[^cite_note-MassTax2-293] An unusual provision allows filers to voluntarily pay at the pre-referendum 5.85% income tax rate, which is done by between one and two thousand taxpayers per year.[^cite_note-294]

The state imposes a 6.25% [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax)[^cite_note-MassTaxes1-292] on retail sales of tangible personal property—except for groceries, clothing (up to \$175.00), and periodicals.[^cite_note-SalesTax1-295] The sales tax is charged on clothing that costs more than \$175.00, for the amount exceeding \$175.00.[^cite_note-SalesTax1-295] Massachusetts also charges a [use tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_tax) when goods are bought from other states and the vendor does not remit Massachusetts sales tax; taxpayers report and pay this on their income tax forms or dedicated forms, though there are "safe harbor" amounts that can be paid without tallying up actual purchases (except for purchases over \$1,000).[^cite_note-SalesTax1-295] There is no [inheritance tax](5824627.md) and limited Massachusetts [estate tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_tax) related to federal estate tax collection.[^cite_note-MassTax2-293]

### Energy

Further information: [Solar power in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_Massachusetts), [Wind power in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power_in_Massachusetts), and [Category\:Energy in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Energy_in_Massachusetts)

Massachusetts's [electricity generation](9540.md) market was made competitive in 1998, enabling retail customers to change suppliers without changing utility companies.[^cite_note-296] In 2018, Massachusetts consumed 1,459 trillion [BTU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Thermal_Units),[^cite_note-EIAmass-297] making it the seventh-lowest state in terms of consumption of energy per capita, and 31 percent of that energy came from [natural gas](22131.md).[^cite_note-EIAmass-297] In 2014 and 2015, Massachusetts was ranked as the most energy efficient state the United States[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] while Boston is the most efficient city,[^cite_note-300] but it had the fourth-highest average residential retail electricity prices of any state.[^cite_note-EIAmass-297] In 2018, renewable energy was about 7.2 percent of total energy consumed in the state, ranking 34th.[^cite_note-EIAmass-297]

### Power initiatives

Main articles: [Solar power in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_Massachusetts) and [Wind power in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power_in_Massachusetts)

See also: [Plug-in electric vehicles in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_electric_vehicles_in_Massachusetts)

The State of Massachusetts has developed a plethora of incentives to encourage the implementation of [renewable energy](25784.md) and efficient appliances and home facilities. The Mass Save program, formed in conjunction with the State by several companies that provide power and gas in Massachusetts, provides homeowners and renters with monetary incentives to [retrofit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retrofit) their homes with efficient HVAC equipment and other household appliances. Appliances such as water heaters, air conditioners, washers and driers, and heat pumps are eligible for rebates in order to incentivize change.[^cite_note-301]

The concept of MassSave was created in 2008 by the passing of the Green Communities Act of 2008, during [Deval Patrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deval_Patrick)'s tenure as [governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor). The main goal of the Green Communities Act was to reduce the consumption of [fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuels) in the State and to encourage new, more efficient technologies. Among others, one result of this act was a requirement for Program Administrators of utilities to invest in saving energy, as opposed to purchasing and generating additional energy where economically feasible. In Massachusetts, eleven Program Administrators, including [Eversource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eversource), [National Grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Grid_\(US\)), [Western Massachusetts Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Massachusetts_Electric_Company), Cape Light Compact, Until, and Berkshire Gas, jointly own the rights to this program, in conjunction with the [Department of Energy Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Energy_Resources_\(Massachusetts\)) (DOER) and the Energy Efficiency Advisory Council (EEAC).[^cite_note-302]

The State Revenue Service provides incentives for the installation of [solar panels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_panel). In addition to the Federal Residential [Renewable energy credit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_credit), Massachusetts residents may be eligible for a tax credit of up to 15 percent of the project.[^cite_note-303] Once installed, arrays are eligible for [net metering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_metering).[^cite_note-304] Certain municipalities will offer up to \$1.20 per watt, up to 50 percent of the system's cost on PV arrays 25 kW or less.[^cite_note-305] The Massachusetts Department of Energy Resources also offered low-interest, fixed-rate financing with loan support for low-income residents until December 31, 2020.[^cite_note-306]

As a part of the Massachusetts Department of Energy Resources' effort to incentivize the usage of [renewable energy](25784.md), the Massachusetts Offers Rebates for Electric Vehicles (MOR-EV) initiative was created. With this incentive, residents may qualify for a state-provided incentive of up to \$2,500 for the purchase or lease of an [electric vehicle](279350.md), or \$1,500 for the purchase or lease of a [plug-in hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid) vehicle.[^cite_note-307] This rebate is available in addition to the tax credits offered by the [United States Department of Energy](62866.md) for the purchase of electric and plug-in hybrid vehicles.[^cite_note-308]

For income-eligible residents, Mass Save has partnered with Massachusetts Community Action Program Agencies and Low-Income Energy Affordability Network (LEAN) to offer residents assistance with upgrades to their homes that will result in more efficient energy usage. Residents may qualify for a replacement of their heating system, insulation installation, appliances, and thermostats if they meet the income qualifications provided on Mass Save's website. For residents of 5+ family residential buildings, there are additional income-restricted benefits available through LEAN. If at least 50 percent of the residents of the building qualify as low income, [energy efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efficient_energy_use) improvements like those available through Mass Save are available. Residential structures operated by non-profit organizations, for profit operations, or housing authorities may take advantage of these programs.[^cite_note-309]

In late 2020, the administration of Massachusetts governor [Charlie Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Baker) released a decarbonization roadmap to aim for net-zero greenhouse gas emissions by 2050. The plan calls for major investments in [offshore wind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Offshore_wind) and solar energy. It would also require all new cars sold in the state to be zero-emissions ([electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_cars) or [hydrogen powered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrogen_powered_car)) by 2035.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311]

### Agriculture

Main article: [Agriculture in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Massachusetts)

As of 2022, there were 7,083 farms in Massachusetts whose products sold had a market value of around \$607 million. The farms encompassed a total of 464,451 acres (1,880 km<sup>2</sup>), averaging 66 acres (27 hectares) apiece.[^cite_note-312] [Greenhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse), [floriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floriculture), and [sod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sod) products—including [the ornamental market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ornamental_plant)—make up more than one third of the state's agricultural output.[^cite_note-census-2017-313][^cite_note-Facts-Stats-314] Massachusetts is also the second highest domestic producer of [cranberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranberries).[^cite_note-315] Sweet corn and apples are also produced in large quantities.[^cite_note-Facts-Stats-314] Fruit cultivation is an important part of the state's agricultural revenues,[^cite_note-fruit-UMassExt-316] and Massachusetts is the second-largest cranberry-producing state after [Wisconsin](33127.md).[^cite_note-317]

## Transportation

Main pages: [Massachusetts Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Department_of_Transportation), [Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Bay_Transportation_Authority), and [Category\:Transportation in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Transportation_in_Massachusetts)

See also: [Plug-in electric vehicles in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_electric_vehicles_in_Massachusetts)



The [Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Bay_Transportation_Authority) serves [Greater Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Boston).

For federal funding purposes, Massachusetts has 10 regional [metropolitan planning organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_planning_organization) and three non-metropolitan planning organizations covering the remainder of the state;[^cite_note-318] statewide planning is handled by the [Massachusetts Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Department_of_Transportation). Transportation is the single largest source of greenhouse gas emissions by economic sector in Massachusetts.[^cite_note-319]

### Regional public transportation

The [Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Bay_Transportation_Authority) (MBTA), also known as "The T",[^cite_note-320] operates public transportation in the form of subway,[^cite_note-321] bus,[^cite_note-322] and ferry[^cite_note-323] systems in the [Metro Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Boston) area.

Fifteen other regional transit authorities provide public transportation in the form of bus services in the rest of the state.[^cite_note-324] Four [heritage railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_railway) are also in operation:

- The [Cape Cod Central Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Cod_Central_Railroad), operating from Hyannis to Buzzards Bay

  [^cite_note-325]

- The [Berkshire Scenic Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkshire_Scenic_Railway_Museum), operating from Lee to Great Barrington

  [^cite_note-326]

- [Edaville Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edaville_Railroad) in Carver

  [^cite_note-327]

- The [Lowell National Historical Park Trolley Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Streetcar_Museum) in Lowell

  [^cite_note-328]

### Long-distance rail and bus

[Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) operates several [inter-city rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-city_rail) lines in Massachusetts. Boston's [South Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Station) serves as the terminus for three lines, namely the high-speed *[Acela Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela_Express)*, which links to cities such as [Providence](19356538.md), [New Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut), [New York City](645042.md), and eventually Washington DC; the *[Northeast Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Regional)*, which follows the same route but includes many more stops, and also continues further south to [Newport News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport_News,_Virginia) in Virginia; and the *[Lake Shore Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Shore_Limited)*, which runs westward to [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester,_Massachusetts), [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Massachusetts), and eventually [Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago,_Illinois).[^cite_note-amtrak-329][^cite_note-330] Boston's other major station, [North Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Station), serves as the southern terminus for Amtrak's *[Downeaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downeaster_\(train\))*, which connects to [Portland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portland,_Maine) and [Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brunswick,_Maine) in Maine.[^cite_note-amtrak-329]

Outside of Boston, Amtrak connects several cities across Massachusetts, along the aforementioned *Acela*, *Northeast Regional*, *Lake Shore Limited*, and *Downeaster* lines, as well as other routes in central and western Massachusetts. The [Amtrak *Hartford Line*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Hartford_Line) connects [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Massachusetts) to [New Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut), operated in conjunction with the [Connecticut Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut_Department_of_Transportation), and the *[Valley Flyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valley_Flyer)* runs a similar route but continues further north to [Greenfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenfield,_Massachusetts). Several stations in western Massachusetts are also served by the *[Vermonter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vermonter_\(train\))*, which connects [St. Albans, Vermont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Albans_station_\(Vermont\)) to Washington DC.[^cite_note-amtrak-329]

Amtrak carries more passengers between Boston and New York than all airlines combined (about 54% of market share in 2012),[^cite_note-331] but service between other cities is less frequent. There, more frequent intercity service is provided by private bus carriers, with the largest being [Peter Pan Bus Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Pan_Bus_Lines), [Greyhound Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greyhound_Lines), and [FlixBus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FlixBus). Various [Chinatown bus lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown_bus_lines) depart for New York from South Station in Boston.[^cite_note-332]

[MBTA Commuter Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBTA_Commuter_Rail) services run throughout the larger Greater Boston area, including service to [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester,_Massachusetts), [Fitchburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fitchburg,_Massachusetts), [Haverhill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haverhill,_Massachusetts), [Newburyport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newburyport,_Massachusetts), [Lowell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowell,_Massachusetts), and [Kingston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston,_Massachusetts).[^cite_note-333] This overlaps with the service areas of neighboring regional transportation authorities. As of the summer of 2013 the [Cape Cod Regional Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Cod_Regional_Transit_Authority) in collaboration with the MBTA and the [Massachusetts Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Department_of_Transportation) (MassDOT) is operating the [CapeFLYER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CapeFLYER) providing passenger rail service between Boston and Cape Cod.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335]

### Ferry

Most ports north of Cape Cod are served by Boston Harbor Cruises, which operates [ferry services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBTA_ferry) in and around [Greater Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Boston) under contract with the [Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Bay_Transportation_Authority). Several routes connect the downtown area with [Hingham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hingham,_Massachusetts), [Hull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hull,_Massachusetts), [Winthrop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winthrop,_Massachusetts), [Salem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem,_Massachusetts), [Logan Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_Airport), [Charlestown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlestown,_Massachusetts), and some of the islands located within the harbor. The same company also operates seasonal service between [Boston](24437894.md) and [Provincetown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincetown).[^cite_note-336]

On the southern shore of the state, several different passenger ferry lines connect [Martha's Vineyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martha's_Vineyard) to ports along the mainland, including [Woods Hole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woods_Hole,_Massachusetts), [Hyannis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyannis,_Massachusetts), [New Bedford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Bedford,_Massachusetts), and [Falmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falmouth,_Massachusetts), all in Massachusetts, as well as [North Kingstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Kingstown,_Rhode_Island) in Rhode Island, [Highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highlands,_New_Jersey) in New Jersey, and [New York City](645042.md) in New York.[^cite_note-337] Similarly, several different lines connect [Nantucket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantucket) to ports including Hyannis, New Bedford, [Harwich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harwich,_Massachusetts), and New York City.[^cite_note-338] Service between the two islands is also offered. The dominant companies serving these routes include [The Steamship Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Steamship_Authority), [Hy-Line Cruises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hy-Line_Cruises), and [Seastreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seastreak), the former of which regulates all passenger services in the region and is also the only company permitted to offer freight ferry services to the islands.[^cite_note-339]

Other ferry connections in the state include a water taxi connecting various points in [Fall River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_River,_Massachusetts),[^cite_note-340] seasonal ferry service connecting [Plymouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth,_Massachusetts) to Provincetown,[^cite_note-341] and a service between New Bedford and [Cuttyhunk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuttyhunk).[^cite_note-342]

### Rail freight

See also: [List of Massachusetts railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Massachusetts_railroads)

As of 2018, a number of [freight railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_freight_transport) were operating in Massachusetts, with Class I railroad [CSX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSX_Transportation) being the largest carrier, and another Class 1, [Norfolk Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Southern) serving the state via its [Pan Am Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan_Am_Southern) joint partnership. Several regional and short line railroads also provide service and connect with other railroads.[^cite_note-343] Massachusetts has a total of 1,110 miles (1,790 km) of freight trackage in operation.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345]

### Air service

Further information: [List of airports in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Massachusetts)



[Logan International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_International_Airport) in Boston is the largest passenger volume airport in New England.

[Boston Logan International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_International_Airport) served 33.5 million passengers in 2015 (up from 31.6 million in 2014)[^cite_note-LoganTraffic-275] through 103 [gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gate_\(airport\)).[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-massport-347] Logan, [Hanscom Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanscom_Field) in [Bedford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedford,_Massachusetts), and [Worcester Regional Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester_Regional_Airport) are operated by [Massport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massport), an independent state transportation agency.[^cite_note-massport-347] Massachusetts has 39 public-use airfields[^cite_note-348] and more than 200 private landing spots.[^cite_note-349] Some airports receive funding from the Aeronautics Division of the [Massachusetts Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Department_of_Transportation) and the [Federal Aviation Administration](11186.md); the FAA is also the primary regulator of Massachusetts air travel.[^cite_note-350]

### Roads



Prominent roads and cities in Massachusetts

There are a total of 36,800 miles (59,200 km) of [interstates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_highway) and other highways in Massachusetts.[^cite_note-351] [Interstate 90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Turnpike) (I-90, also known as the Massachusetts Turnpike), is the longest interstate in Massachusetts. The route travels 136 mi (219 km) generally west to east, entering Massachusetts at the New York state line in the town of [West Stockbridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Stockbridge,_Massachusetts), and passes just north of [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Massachusetts), just south of [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester,_Massachusetts) and through [Framingham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Framingham,_Massachusetts) before terminating near Logan International Airport in Boston.[^cite_note-352]

Other major interstates include [I-91](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_91_in_Massachusetts), which travels generally north and south along the [Connecticut River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut_River); [I-93](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_93_in_Massachusetts), which travels north and south through central Boston, then passes through [Methuen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methuen,_Massachusetts) before entering New Hampshire; and [I-95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_95_in_Massachusetts), which connects [Providence, Rhode Island](19356538.md) with Greater Boston, forming a partial [loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beltway) [concurrent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concurrency_\(road\)) with [Route 128](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Route_128) around the more urbanized areas before continuing north along the coast into New Hampshire.[^cite_note-353]

[I-495](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_495_\(Massachusetts\)) forms a wide loop around the outer edge of Greater Boston. Other major interstates in Massachusetts include [I-291](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_291_\(Massachusetts\)), [I-391](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_391_\(Massachusetts\)), [I-84](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_84_in_Massachusetts), [I-195](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_195_in_Massachusetts), [I-395](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_395_in_Massachusetts), [I-290](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_290_\(Massachusetts\)), and [I-190](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_190_\(Massachusetts\)). Major non-interstate highways in Massachusetts include [U.S. Routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Numbered_Highways) [1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_1_in_Massachusetts), [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_3_in_Massachusetts), [5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_5_in_Massachusetts), [6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_6_in_Massachusetts), [7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_7_in_Massachusetts), [20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_20_in_Massachusetts), and [44](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_44_in_Massachusetts), and state routes [2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Route_2), [2A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Route_2A), [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Route_3), [9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Route_9), [24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Route_24), and [128](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Route_128). A great majority of interstates in Massachusetts were constructed during the mid-20th century, and at times were controversial, particularly the intent [to route I-95 northeastwards from Providence, Rhode Island, directly through central Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_Corridor_\(Massachusetts\)), first proposed in 1948. Opposition to continued construction grew, and in 1970 Governor [Francis W. Sargent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_W._Sargent) issued a general prohibition on most further freeway construction within the I-95/Route 128 loop in the Boston area.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrownTager2000283–284-354] A massive undertaking to bring I-93 underground in downtown Boston, called the [Big Dig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Dig), brought the city's highway system under public scrutiny for its high cost and construction quality.[^cite_note-BigDig1-144]

## Government and politics



The [Massachusetts State House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_State_House) is topped with a golden dome, and faces [Boston Common](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Common) on [Beacon Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beacon_Hill,_Boston).

Massachusetts has a long political history; earlier political structures included the [Mayflower Compact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayflower_Compact) of 1620, the separate [Massachusetts Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Bay_Colony) and [Plymouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth_Colony) colonies, and the combined colonial [Province of Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Massachusetts). The [Massachusetts Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Constitution) was ratified in 1780 while the [Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War) was in progress, four years after the [Articles of Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articles_of_Confederation) was drafted, and eight years before the present [United States Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Constitution) was ratified on June 21, 1788. Drafted by [John Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adams), the Massachusetts Constitution is the oldest functioning written constitution in continuous effect in the world.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] It has been amended 121 times, most recently in 2022.[^cite_note-358]

Massachusetts politics since the second half of the 20th century have generally been dominated by the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Democratic_Party), and the state has a reputation for being the most [liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_liberalism_in_the_United_States) state in the country.[^cite_note-359] In 1974, [Elaine Noble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elaine_Noble) became [the first](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_first_LGBT_holders_of_political_offices) openly lesbian or gay candidate elected to a state legislature in US history.[^cite_note-360] The state's [12th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_12th_congressional_district) elected the first openly gay member of the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md), [Gerry Studds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerry_Studds), in 1972[^cite_note-361] and in 2004, Massachusetts became the first state to allow [same-sex marriage](92656.md).[^cite_note-CNNmarriage-65]

In 2006, Massachusetts became the first state to approve a law that provided for nearly universal healthcare.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-Mandatory-363] Massachusetts has a pro-[sanctuary city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctuary_city) law.[^cite_note-364] As of 2024, Massachusetts has a Democratic Governor, two Democratic Senators, and all nine Congressional Representatives are Democrats. Massachusetts is a blue state; [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) was the last Republican to win the state in [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_United_States_presidential_election).

In a 2020 study, Massachusetts was ranked as the 11th easiest state for citizens to vote in.[^cite_note-365]

### Government

Main article: [Government of Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Massachusetts)

See also: [Commonwealth (U.S. state)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_\(U.S._state\))



[Maura Healey](41875710.md) ([D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_\(Massachusetts\))) is the 73rd [Governor of Massachusetts](252510.md).

The [Government of Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_government) is divided into three branches: executive, legislative, and judicial. The [governor of Massachusetts](252510.md) heads the executive branch. Legislative authority vests in a separate but coequal legislature. Judicial power is constitutionally guaranteed to the independent judicial branch.[^cite_note-366]

#### Executive branch

As chief executive, the governor is responsible for signing or vetoing legislation, filling judicial and agency appointments, granting pardons, preparing an annual budget, and commanding the [Massachusetts National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_National_Guard).[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] Massachusetts governors, unlike those of most other states, are addressed as His/Her Excellency.[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] The governor is [Maura Healey](41875710.md) and the incumbent lieutenant governor is [Kim Driscoll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Driscoll). The governor conducts the affairs of state alongside a separate [Governor's Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Governor's_Council) made up of the [lieutenant governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_Governor_of_Massachusetts) and eight separately elected councilors.[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] The council is charged by the state constitution with reviewing and confirming gubernatorial appointments and pardons, approving disbursements out of the state treasury, and certifying elections, among other duties.[^cite_note-MassPol1-367]

The executive branch includes four independently elected constitutional officers: a [secretary of the commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Secretary_of_the_Commonwealth), an [attorney general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Attorney_General), a [state treasurer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treasurer_and_Receiver-General_of_Massachusetts), and a [state auditor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_State_Auditor). The commonwealth's incumbent constitutional officers are respectively [William F. Galvin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_F._Galvin), [Andrea Campbell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Campbell), [Deb Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deb_Goldberg) and [Diana DiZoglio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana_DiZoglio), all [Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Democratic_Party). In accordance with state statute, the secretary of the commonwealth administers elections, regulates lobbyists and the securities industry, registers corporations, serves as register of deeds for the entire state, and preserves public records as keeper of the [state seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seal_of_Massachusetts).[^cite_note-368]

The attorney general provides legal services to state agencies, combats fraud and corruption, investigates and prosecutes crimes, and enforces consumer protection, environment, labor, and civil rights laws as Massachusetts chief lawyer and law enforcement officer.[^cite_note-369] The state treasurer manages the state's cash flow, debt, and investments as chief financial officer. The state auditor conducts audits, investigations, and studies as chief audit executive in order to promote government accountability and transparency and improve state agency financial management, legal compliance, and performance.[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371]

#### Legislative branch

The [Massachusetts House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_House_of_Representatives) and [Massachusetts Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Senate) comprise the legislature of Massachusetts, known as the [Massachusetts General Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_General_Court).[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] The House consists of 160 members while the Senate has 40 members.[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] Leaders of the House and Senate are chosen by the members of those bodies; the leader of the House is known as the Speaker while the leader of the Senate is known as the President.[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] Each branch consists of several committees.[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] Members of both bodies are elected to two-year terms.[^cite_note-372]

#### Judicial branch

The [Massachusetts Supreme Judicial Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Supreme_Judicial_Court) (a chief justice and six associates) are appointed by the governor and confirmed by the Governor's Council, as are all other judges in the state.[^cite_note-MassPol1-367]

Federal court cases are heard in the [United States District Court for the District of Massachusetts](915466.md), and appeals are heard by the [United States Court of Appeals for the First Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_First_Circuit).[^cite_note-373]

#### Federal representation

The congressional delegation from Massachusetts is entirely [Democratic](5043544.md).[^cite_note-374] The [senators](24909346.md) are [Elizabeth Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Warren) and [Ed Markey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Markey) while the [representatives](19468510.md) are [Richard Neal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Neal) ([1st](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_1st_congressional_district)), [Jim McGovern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_McGovern_\(congressman\)) ([2nd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_2nd_congressional_district)), [Lori Trahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lori_Trahan) ([3rd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_3rd_congressional_district)), [Jake Auchincloss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Auchincloss) ([4th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_4th_congressional_district)), [Katherine Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katherine_Clark) ([5th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_5th_congressional_district)), [Seth Moulton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seth_Moulton) ([6th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_6th_congressional_district)), [Ayanna Pressley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayanna_Pressley) ([7th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_7th_congressional_district)), [Stephen Lynch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Lynch_\(politician\)) ([8th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_8th_congressional_district)), and [Bill Keating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Keating_\(politician\)) ([9th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts's_9th_congressional_district)).[^cite_note-375]

In U.S. presidential elections since 2012, Massachusetts has been allotted 11 votes in the [electoral college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Electoral_College), out of a total of 538.[^cite_note-376] Like most states, Massachusetts's electoral votes are granted in a winner-take-all system.[^cite_note-377]

### Politics

Main articles: [Politics of Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Massachusetts) and [Political party strength in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_party_strength_in_Massachusetts)

See also: [United States presidential elections in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_elections_in_Massachusetts)

Two older men and an older woman stand in a crowd with signs reading "Joe Kennedy for Congress".

Representative [Joe Kennedy III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Kennedy_III), Senator [Elizabeth Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Warren), and former representative [Barney Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barney_Frank) attended the Boston Pride Parade in 2012.

Once a [Republican](32070.md)-leaning state, for more than 70 years Massachusetts has been [largely dominated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_states_and_blue_states) by [Democrats](5043544.md); the [1952 victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_election_in_Massachusetts,_1952) of [John F. Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_F._Kennedy) over incumbent Senator [Henry Cabot Lodge Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Cabot_Lodge_Jr.) is seen as a watershed moment in this transformation. His younger brother [Edward M. Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Kennedy) held that seat until his death from a brain tumor in 2009.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrownTager2000310-378] Since the 1950s, Massachusetts has gained a reputation as being a politically liberal state and is often used as an archetype of [modern liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_liberalism_in_the_United_States), hence the phrase "[Massachusetts liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_liberal)".[^cite_note-379]

Massachusetts is one of the most Democratic states in the country. The Democratic Party predominates throughout, except for a handful of Republican-leaning towns in the central and southern parts of the state. Republicans once dominated in the northern and western suburbs of Boston; however, both areas heavily swung Democratic in the era of [Donald Trump](4848272.md). The state as a whole has not given its [Electoral College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_College_\(United_States\)) votes to a Republican in a [presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_election) since [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) carried it in [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_United_States_presidential_election), and not a single county has voted for a Republican presidential candidate since [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_United_States_presidential_election_in_Massachusetts). While supporting Reagan twice, Massachusetts provided his smallest margins of victory in both the [1980](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_presidential_election)[^cite_note-380] and 1984 elections.[^cite_note-381] Massachusetts had been the only state to vote for Democrat [George McGovern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_McGovern) in the [1972 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_United_States_Presidential_Election). In [2020, Biden received 65.6% of the vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_election_in_Massachusetts), the best performance in over 50 years for a Democrat.[^cite_note-382]

Democrats have an absolute grip on the Massachusetts congressional delegation; there are no Republicans elected to serve at the federal level. Both Senators and all nine Representatives are Democrats; only one Republican (former Senator [Scott Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Brown_\(politician\))) has been elected to either house of Congress from Massachusetts since 1994. Massachusetts is the most populous state to be represented in the [United States Congress](31756.md) entirely by a single party.[^cite_note-383]

After the 2018 elections, the Democratic Party held a super-majority over the Republican Party in [both chambers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameralism) of the [Massachusetts General Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_General_Court) (state legislature). Out of the [state house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_House_of_Representatives)'s 160 seats, Democrats hold 127 seats (79%) compared to the Republican Party's 32 seats (20%), an [independent sits in the remaining one](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susannah_Whipps),[^cite_note-384] and 37 out of the 40 seats in the [state senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_state_senate) (92.5%) belong to the Democratic Party compared to the Republican Party's three seats (7.5%).[^cite_note-385] Both houses of the legislature have had Democratic majorities since the 1950s.[^cite_note-386]

| Party registration as of February 2025:[^cite_note-387] |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

|  | [Unenrolled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_voter) | 3,254,435 | 64.75% |

Despite the state's general Democratic lean, Massachusetts has frequently elected Republicans as [Governor](252510.md): only two Democrats ([Deval Patrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deval_Patrick) and [Maura Healey](41875710.md)) have won the governorship since 1991, and among gubernatorial election results from 2002 to 2022, Republican nominees garnered 48.4% of the vote compared to 45.7% for Democratic nominees.[^cite_note-Leip,_David-388] These have been considered to be among the most moderate Republican leaders in the nation;[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] they have received higher net favorability ratings from the state's Democrats than Republicans.[^cite_note-391]

A number of contemporary national political issues have been influenced by events in Massachusetts, such as the decision in 2003 by the state Supreme Judicial Court [allowing same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Massachusetts)[^cite_note-392] and [a 2006 bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_2006_Health_Reform_Statute) which mandated health insurance for all Massachusetts residents.[^cite_note-Mandatory-363] In 2008, Massachusetts voters passed [an initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Sensible_Marijuana_Policy_Initiative) decriminalizing possession of small amounts of [marijuana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marijuana).[^cite_note-393] Voters in Massachusetts also approved a ballot measure in 2012 that legalized the medical use of marijuana.[^cite_note-394] Following the approval of a ballot question endorsing legalization in 2016, Massachusetts began issuing licenses for the regulated sale of recreational marijuana in June 2018. The licensed sale of recreational marijuana became legal on July 1, 2018; however, the lack of state-approved testing facilities prevented the sale of any product for several weeks.[^cite_note-395] However, in 2020, a ballot initiative to implement [Ranked-Choice Voting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Massachusetts_Question_2) failed, despite being championed by many [progressives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressivism_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-396]

Massachusetts is one of the most [pro-choice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion-rights_movements) states in the Union. A 2014 [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) poll found that 74% of Massachusetts residents supported the right to an [abortion](765.md) in all/most cases, making Massachusetts the most pro-choice state in the United States.[^cite_note-397]

In 2020, the state legislature overrode Governor [Charlie Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Baker)'s veto of the ROE Act, a controversial law that codified existing abortion laws in case the [Supreme Court](31737.md) overturned [Roe v. Wade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe_v._Wade), dropped the age of parental consent for those seeking an abortion from 18 to 16, and legalized abortion after 24 weeks, if a fetus had fatal anomalies, or "to preserve the patient's physical or mental health."[^cite_note-398]

The 2023 *American Values Atlas* by *[Public Religion Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Religion_Research_Institute)* found that [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_United_States) is supported near-universally by Massachusettsans.[^cite_note-399]

## Cities, towns, and counties

Main articles: [Government of Massachusetts § County Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Massachusetts#County_government), and [Government of Massachusetts § Municipal Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Massachusetts#Municipal_government)

As of 2023,[^cite_note-MMA-400] there are [292 towns and 59 cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Massachusetts) in Massachusetts. Over time, many towns have voted to become cities; 14 municipalities still refer to themselves as "towns" even though they have a city form of government.[^cite_note-MMA-400] (See [Administrative divisions of Massachusetts § Form of government: city vs. town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Massachusetts#Form_of_government:_city_vs._town).) Massachusetts, along with the five other [New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England) states, features the local governmental structure known as [the New England town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_town).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESokolow1997293–6-401] In this structure, incorporated towns—as opposed to townships or counties—hold many of the responsibilities and powers of local government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESokolow1997293–6-401]

The fourteen counties[^cite_note-MassCities1-402] of Massachusetts, moving roughly from west to east, are [Berkshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkshire_County,_Massachusetts), [Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_County,_Massachusetts), [Hampshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampshire_County,_Massachusetts), [Hampden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampden_County,_Massachusetts), [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester_County,_Massachusetts), [Middlesex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middlesex_County,_Massachusetts), [Essex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essex_County,_Massachusetts), [Suffolk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suffolk_County,_Massachusetts), [Norfolk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_County,_Massachusetts), [Bristol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol_County,_Massachusetts), [Plymouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth_County,_Massachusetts), [Barnstable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barnstable_County,_Massachusetts), [Dukes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dukes_County,_Massachusetts), and [Nantucket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantucket_County,_Massachusetts). Most of the county governments were abolished by the state of Massachusetts beginning in 1997 including Middlesex County,[^cite_note-MassPol1-367] the largest county in the state by population.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404] The voters of these now-defunct counties elect only Sheriffs and Registers of Deeds, who are part of the state government. Other counties have been reorganized, and a few still retain county councils.[^cite_note-405]

Boston is the state capital in Massachusetts. The population of the city proper is 692,600,[^cite_note-406] and [Greater Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Boston), with a population of 4,873,019, is the 11th largest [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area) in the nation.[^cite_note-407] Other cities with a population over 100,000 include [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester,_Massachusetts), [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Massachusetts), [Lowell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowell,_Massachusetts), [Cambridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge,_Massachusetts), [Brockton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brockton,_Massachusetts), [Quincy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy,_Massachusetts), [New Bedford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Bedford,_Massachusetts), and [Lynn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynn,_Massachusetts). [Plymouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth,_Massachusetts) is the largest municipality in the state by land area, followed by [Middleborough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middleborough,_Massachusetts).[^cite_note-MassCities1-402]

## Culture

Massachusetts has contributed to American arts and culture. Drawing from its [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) and [Yankee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yankee) roots, along with later immigrant groups, Massachusetts has had several writers, artists, and musicians. It has major museums and important historical sites, and the state's history and heritage are celebrated by events and festivals.[^cite_note-408]

Massachusetts became an early center of the [Transcendentalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transcendentalism) movement, which emphasized intuition, emotion, human individuality and a deeper connection with nature.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldfieldAbbottAndersonArgersinger1998254-131] [Ralph Waldo Emerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ralph_Waldo_Emerson), who was born in Boston but spent much of his later life in [Concord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concord,_Massachusetts), largely created the philosophy with his 1836 work [*Nature*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_\(essay\)), and continued to be a key figure in the movement for the remainder of his life. Emerson's friend, [Henry David Thoreau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_David_Thoreau), who was also involved in Transcendentalism, recorded his year spent alone in a small cabin at nearby [Walden Pond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walden_Pond) in the 1854 work *[Walden; or, Life in the Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walden;_or,_Life_in_the_Woods)*.[^cite_note-409]

Other famous authors and poets born or strongly associated with Massachusetts include [Anne Bradstreet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne_Bradstreet), [Nathaniel Hawthorne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathaniel_Hawthorne), [Louisa May Alcott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisa_May_Alcott), [Robert Frost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Frost), [Emily Dickinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Dickinson), [Henry Wadsworth Longfellow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Wadsworth_Longfellow), [Edith Wharton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edith_Wharton), [E.E. Cummings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.e._cummings), [Herman Melville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herman_Melville), [W.E.B. Du Bois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W.E.B._Du_Bois), [Sylvia Plath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvia_Plath), [Elizabeth Bishop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Bishop), [John Updike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Updike), [Anne Sexton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne_Sexton), [H.P. Lovecraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H.P._Lovecraft), [Edgar Allan Poe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgar_Allan_Poe), [Helen Hunt Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helen_Hunt_Jackson), [Khalil Gibran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalil_Gibran), [Mary Higgins Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Higgins_Clark), [Amelia Atwater-Rhodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amelia_Atwater-Rhodes), [Jack Kerouac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Kerouac) and [Theodor Seuss Geisel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Seuss), better known as "Dr. Seuss".[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411][^cite_note-MassMisc-412] Famous painters from Massachusetts include [Winslow Homer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winslow_Homer) and [Norman Rockwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_Rockwell);[^cite_note-MassMisc-412] many of the latter's works are on display at the [Norman Rockwell Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_Rockwell_Museum) in [Stockbridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockbridge,_Massachusetts).[^cite_note-413]

Massachusetts is an important center for the performing arts. It has the [Boston Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Symphony_Orchestra), [Boston Pops Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Pops_Orchestra)[^cite_note-BostonArts1-414] the [Cape Cod Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Cod_Symphony_Orchestra) in [Barnstable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barnstable,_Massachusetts), the New Bedford Symphony Orchestra,[^cite_note-415] and the [Springfield Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_Symphony_Orchestra).[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] [Tanglewood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanglewood) in western Massachusetts is a music venue that hosts the [Tanglewood Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanglewood_Music_Festival) and [Tanglewood Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanglewood_Jazz_Festival), and is the annual host for the Boston Symphony Orchestra.[^cite_note-418]

Other performing arts and theater organizations include the [Boston Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Ballet), the [Boston Lyric Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Lyric_Opera),[^cite_note-BostonArts1-414] and the [Lenox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenox,_Massachusetts)-based [Shakespeare & Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakespeare_&_Company_\(Massachusetts\)). Massachusetts has produced musicians of many contemporary genres, such as the [classic rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_rock) bands [Aerosmith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerosmith) and [Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_\(band\)), the proto-punk band [the Modern Lovers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Modern_Lovers), the [new wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_wave_music) band [the Cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Cars), and the [alternative rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_rock) band [Pixies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pixies_\(band\)).[^cite_note-419] The state has also been the birthplace of the rock bands [Staind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staind), [Godsmack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Godsmack), and [Highly Suspect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highly_Suspect), since these bands all were formed in Massachusetts cities such [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_\(Massachusetts\)), [Lawrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence,_Massachusetts), and [Cape Cod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Cod) respectively.[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422] Film events in the state include the [Boston Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Film_Festival), the [Boston International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_International_Film_Festival), and a number of smaller film festivals in various cities throughout Massachusetts.[^cite_note-423]

Massachusetts has many museums and historical sites, including the [Clark Art Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clark_Art_Institute), the [Museum of Fine Arts, Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Fine_Arts,_Boston), the [Institute of Contemporary Art, Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Contemporary_Art,_Boston), and the [DeCordova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeCordova_Museum) contemporary art and sculpture museum in [Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln,_Massachusetts),[^cite_note-424] and the [Maria Mitchell Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Mitchell_Association) in [Nantucket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantucket) includes several observatories, museums, and an aquarium.[^cite_note-425] Historically themed museums and sites such as the [Springfield Armory National Historic Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_Armory_National_Historic_Site) in [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Massachusetts),[^cite_note-NPS1-162] Boston's [Freedom Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Trail), and nearby [Minute Man National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minute_Man_National_Historical_Park), both of which preserve many sites important during the [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution),[^cite_note-NPS1-162][^cite_note-426] the [Lowell National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowell_National_Historical_Park), which focuses on some of the earliest mills and canals of the [Industrial Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Revolution) in the US,[^cite_note-NPS1-162] the [Black Heritage Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Heritage_Trail_\(Boston\)) in Boston, which includes important African-American and abolitionist sites in Boston,[^cite_note-427] and the [New Bedford Whaling National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Bedford_Whaling_National_Historical_Park)[^cite_note-NPS1-162] all showcase various periods of Massachusetts's history. [Plymouth Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth_Rock), marks the disembarkation site of the *[Mayflower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayflower)* [Pilgrims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilgrim_\(Plymouth_Colony\)) who founded [Plymouth Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth_Colony) in December 1620.

[Plimoth Patuxet Museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plimoth_Patuxet) and [Old Sturbridge Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Sturbridge_Village) are two [open-air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-air_museum) or "living" museums in Massachusetts, recreating life as it was in the 17th and early 19th centuries, respectively.[^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429]

Boston's annual [St. Patrick's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Patrick's_Day) parade and "Harborfest", a week-long [Fourth of July](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_of_July) celebration featuring a fireworks display and concert by the Boston Pops as well as a turnaround cruise in Boston Harbor by the [USS *Constitution*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Constitution), are popular events.[^cite_note-430] The [New England Summer Nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_Summer_Nationals), an [auto show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_show) in Worcester, draws tens of thousands of attendees every year.[^cite_note-431] The [Boston Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Marathon) is also a popular event in the state drawing more than 30,000 runners and tens of thousands of spectators annually.[^cite_note-432]

Long-distance hiking trails in Massachusetts include the [Appalachian Trail](87009.md), the [New England National Scenic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_National_Scenic_Trail), the [Metacomet-Monadnock Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metacomet-Monadnock_Trail), the [Midstate Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midstate_Trail_\(Massachusetts\)), and the Bay Circuit Trail.[^cite_note-433] Other outdoor recreational activities in Massachusetts include sailing and yachting, freshwater and deep-sea fishing,[^cite_note-434] [whale watching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whale_watching),[^cite_note-435] downhill and cross-country skiing,[^cite_note-436] and hunting.[^cite_note-437]

Massachusetts is one of the states with the largest percentage of [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic). It has many sanctuaries such as the [National Shrine of The Divine Mercy (Stockbridge, Massachusetts)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Shrine_of_The_Divine_Mercy_\(Stockbridge,_Massachusetts\)).[^cite_note-438]

- The site of Henry David Thoreau's cabin is at Walden Pond in Concord.

  The site of [Henry David Thoreau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_David_Thoreau)'s cabin is at [Walden Pond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walden_Pond) in [Concord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concord,_Massachusetts).

- Massachusetts hosts many prestigious art museums, such as the Clark Art Institute.

  Massachusetts hosts many prestigious art museums, such as the [Clark Art Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clark_Art_Institute).

- An outdoor dance performance at Jacob's Pillow in Becket.

  An outdoor dance performance at [Jacob's Pillow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob's_Pillow_Dance) in [Becket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Becket,_Massachusetts).

- The USS Constitution fires a salute during its annual Fourth of July turnaround cruise.

  The USS *[Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Constitution)* fires a [salute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_salute) during its annual Fourth of July turnaround cruise.

- The Old Ship Church in Hingham was built in 1681, and is the oldest church in America in continuous ecclesiastical use. The Massachusetts population has since become one of the most irreligious of US states.

  The [Old Ship Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Ship_Church) in [Hingham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hingham,_Massachusetts) was built in 1681, and is the oldest church in America in continuous ecclesiastical use.

  [^cite_note-439]

  &#x20;The Massachusetts population has since become one of the most [irreligious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion) of US states.

  [^cite_note-440]
