  Massachusetts has the largest population of the [New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England) states. New Englander culture and identity remains strong in Massachusetts ([Flag of New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_New_England) pictured above).

  [^cite_note-441]

## Media

See also: [List of television stations in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_television_stations_in_Massachusetts), [List of newspapers in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Massachusetts), and [List of radio stations in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_radio_stations_in_Massachusetts)

There are two major television media markets located in Massachusetts. The Boston/Manchester market is the fifth-largest in the United States.[^cite_note-442] The other market surrounds the Springfield area.[^cite_note-443] [WGBH-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGBH-TV) in Boston is a major public television station and produces national programs such as [*Nova*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nova_\(American_TV_series\)), [*Frontline*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontline_\(U.S._TV_series\)), and *[American Experience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Experience)*.[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445]

*[The Boston Globe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Boston_Globe)*, *[Boston Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Herald)*, *[Springfield Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_Republican)*, and the *[Worcester Telegram & Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester_Telegram_&_Gazette)* are Massachusetts's largest daily newspapers.[^cite_note-446] In addition, there are many community dailies and weeklies. The [Associated Press](18935732.md) maintains a bureau in Boston, and local [news wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_wire) the [State House News Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_House_News_Service) feeds coverage of state government to other Massachusetts media outlets. There are a number of major [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AM_broadcasting) and [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FM_broadcasting) stations which serve Massachusetts,[^cite_note-447] along with many more regional and community-based stations. Some colleges and universities also operate campus television and radio stations, and print their own newspapers.[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450]

## Health

See also: [List of hospitals in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_hospitals_in_Massachusetts), [Massachusetts health care reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_health_care_reform), and [Governorship of Mitt Romney § Health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governorship_of_Mitt_Romney#Health_care)



A map of the average Medicare reimbursement per enrollee by county, 2016.

[^cite_note-451]

Massachusetts generally ranks highly among states in most health and disease prevention categories. In 2015, the [United Health Foundation](1845551.md) ranked the state as third-healthiest overall.[^cite_note-452] Massachusetts has the most doctors per 100,000 residents (435.38),[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] the second-lowest [infant mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate) (3.8),[^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456] and the lowest percentage of uninsured residents (children as well as the total population).[^cite_note-457][^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459]

In 2018, commonwealth residents had an average life expectancy of 80.41 years, the [fifth-longest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_life_expectancy) in the country.[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461] In 2015, 36.1% of the population was overweight and 24.4% were obese.[^cite_note-CDC1-462] Massachusetts ranks sixth-highest in the percentage of residents who are considered neither obese nor overweight (39.5%).[^cite_note-CDC1-462] In 2018, Massachusetts ranked above average in the prevalence of [binge drinking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binge_drinking), which is the 20th-highest in the country.[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464]

The nation's first [Marine Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Hospital_Service) was erected by federal order in Boston in 1799.[^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466] There are 143 hospitals in Massachusetts.[^cite_note-467] In 2015, [Massachusetts General Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_General_Hospital) was ranked in the top three in two health care specialties.[^cite_note-468] Massachusetts General Hospital was founded in 1811 and serves as the largest teaching hospital for nearby [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University).[^cite_note-469]

Massachusetts is a center for medical education and research, including Harvard affiliates [Brigham and Women's Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigham_and_Women's_Hospital), [Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beth_Israel_Deaconess_Medical_Center), and [Dana–Farber Cancer Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana%E2%80%93Farber_Cancer_Institute)[^cite_note-470] and the [New England Baptist Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_Baptist_Hospital), [Tufts Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tufts_Medical_Center), and [Boston Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Medical_Center) which is the primary teaching hospital for [Boston University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_University).[^cite_note-471] The [University of Massachusetts Chan Medical School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Massachusetts_Chan_Medical_School) is located in [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester,_Massachusetts).[^cite_note-472] The [Massachusetts College of Pharmacy and Health Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_College_of_Pharmacy_and_Health_Sciences) has two of its three campuses in Boston and Worcester.[^cite_note-473]

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Massachusetts)



Gillette Stadium in [Foxborough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxborough,_Massachusetts) is the home venue for the [New England Patriots](21719.md) (NFL) and the [New England Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_Revolution) (MLS).

Massachusetts is home to five major league professional sports teams: eighteen-time [NBA Champions](890793.md) [Boston Celtics](43376.md),[^cite_note-474] nine-time [World Series](23489161.md) winners [Boston Red Sox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Red_Sox),[^cite_note-475] six-time [Stanley Cup](66968.md) winners [Boston Bruins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Bruins),[^cite_note-476] six-time [Super Bowl](27718.md) winners [New England Patriots](21719.md),[^cite_note-477] and five-time [MLS Cup](483246.md) finalists [New England Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_Revolution).[^cite_note-478]

In the late 19th century, the [Olympic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympics) sports of basketball[^cite_note-basketball-479] and volleyball[^cite_note-volleyball-480] were invented in the Western Massachusetts cities of [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Massachusetts)[^cite_note-basketball-479] and [Holyoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holyoke),[^cite_note-volleyball-480] respectively. The [Basketball Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_Hall_of_Fame) is a major tourist destination in the City of Springfield and the [Volleyball Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volleyball_Hall_of_Fame) is located in Holyoke.[^cite_note-volleyball-480] The [American Hockey League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Hockey_League) (AHL), the [NHL](21809.md)'s development league, is headquartered in Springfield.[^cite_note-481]

Several universities in Massachusetts are notable for their collegiate athletics. The state is home to two [Division I FBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_I_Football_Bowl_Subdivision) teams, [Boston College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_College) of the [Atlantic Coast Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Coast_Conference), and FBS [Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_I_FBS_independent_schools) [University of Massachusetts at Amherst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Massachusetts_Amherst). [FCS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Championship_Subdivision) play includes [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University), which competes in the famed [Ivy League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_League), and [College of the Holy Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_the_Holy_Cross) of the [Patriot League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriot_League). [Boston University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_University), [Northeastern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeastern_University_\(Boston,_Massachusetts\)), [UMASS Lowell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Massachusetts_Lowell), [Stonehill College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonehill_College), and [Merrimack College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrimack_College) also participate in Division I athletics.[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-483] Many other Massachusetts colleges compete in lower divisions such as [Division III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_III_\(NCAA\)), where [MIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIT), [Tufts University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tufts_University), [Amherst College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amherst_College), [Williams College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_College), and others field competitive teams.[^cite_note-484]

Massachusetts is also the home of rowing events such as the [Eastern Sprints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Sprints) on [Lake Quinsigamond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Quinsigamond) and the [Head of the Charles Regatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_the_Charles_Regatta).[^cite_note-485] A number of major golf events have taken place in Massachusetts, including nine [U.S. Opens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Open_\(golf\)) and two [Ryder Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryder_Cup).[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487]

Massachusetts has produced several successful Olympians including [Thomas Burke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Burke_\(athlete\)), [James Connolly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Brendan_Connolly), and [John Thomas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thomas_\(athlete\)) ([track and field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Track_and_field_at_the_Summer_Olympics)); [Butch Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butch_Johnson) ([archery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archery_at_the_Summer_Olympics)); [Nancy Kerrigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Kerrigan) ([figure skating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Figure_skating_at_the_Olympic_Games)); [Todd Richards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Richards_\(snowboarder\)) ([snowboarding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowboarding_at_the_Winter_Olympics)); [Albina Osipowich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albina_Osipowich) ([swimming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_at_the_Summer_Olympics)); [Aly Raisman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aly_Raisman) ([gymnastics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gymnastics_at_the_Summer_Olympics)); [Patrick Ewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Ewing) ([basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_at_the_Summer_Olympics)); [Stephen Nedoroscik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Nedoroscik) ([pommel horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pommel_horse_at_the_Olympics)); as well as [Jim Craig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Craig_\(ice_hockey\)), [Mike Eruzione](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Eruzione), [Bill Cleary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Cleary_\(ice_hockey\)), [Keith Tkachuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Tkachuk) ([ice hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_hockey_at_the_Olympic_Games)).[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489]

## Further reading

### Overviews and surveys

- Hall, Donald. ed. *The Encyclopedia of New England* (2005)

- [Works Progress Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Works_Progress_Administration). *Guide to Massachusetts* (1939)

### Secondary sources

- Abrams, Richard M. *Conservatism in a Progressive Era: Massachusetts Politics, 1900–1912* (1964)

- Adams, James Truslow. *Revolutionary New England, 1691–1776* (1923)

- Adams, James Truslow. *New England in the Republic, 1776–1850* (1926)

- Andrews, Charles M. *The Fathers of New England: A Chronicle of the Puritan Commonwealths* (1919), short survey

- Conforti, Joseph A. *Imagining New England: Explorations of Regional Identity from the Pilgrims to the Mid-Twentieth Century* (2001)

- Cumbler, John T. *Reasonable Use: The People, the Environment, and the State, New England, 1790–1930* (1930), environmental history

- Fischer, David Hackett. *Paul Revere's Ride* (1994), 1775 in depth

- Flagg, Charles Allcott, [*A Guide to Massachusetts local history*](https://archive.org/details/aguidetomassach00flaggoog), Salem : Salem Press Company, 1907.

- Green, James R., William F. Hartford, and Tom Juravich. *Commonwealth of Toil: Chapters in the History of Massachusetts Workers and Their Unions* (1996)

- Huthmacher, J. Joseph. *Massachusetts People and Politics, 1919–1933* (1958)

- Labaree, Benjamin Woods. *Colonial Massachusetts: A History* (1979)

- Morison, Samuel Eliot. *The Maritime History of Massachusetts, 1783–1860* (1921)

- Peirce, Neal R. *The New England States: People, Politics, and Power in the Six New England States* (1976), 1960–75 era

- Porter, Susan L. *Women of the Commonwealth: Work, Family, and Social Change in Nineteenth-Century Massachusetts* (1996)

- Sletcher, Michael. *New England* (2004).

- Starkey, Marion L. *The Devil in Massachusetts* (1949), Salem witches

- Tager, Jack, and John W. Ifkovic, eds. *Massachusetts in the Gilded Age: Selected Essays* (1985), ethnic groups

- Zimmerman, Joseph F. *[The New England Town Meeting: Democracy in Action](https://www.questia.com/library/book/the-new-england-town-meeting-democracy-in-action-by-joseph-f-zimmerman.jsp) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20111123155020/http://www.questia.com/library/book/the-new-england-town-meeting-democracy-in-action-by-joseph-f-zimmerman.jsp) November 23, 2011, at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine)* (1999)

Retrieved June 19, 2022. All 2020 data are final. 2020 birth data come from the National Vital Statistics System (NVSS) via CDC WONDER; 2020 death data, including leading causes of death, firearm mortality, homicide, drug overdose mortality, and infant mortality, come from the NVSS via CDC WONDER and rankings and rates are based on 2020 age-adjusted death rates. For more information on age-adjustment, refer to this reportpdf icon. Where ranked, states are categorized from highest rate to lowest rate. Although adjusted for variations in age-distribution and population size, differences by state do not take into account other state specific population characteristics that may affect the level of the birth characteristic or mortality. When the number of deaths or births events is small, differences by state may be unreliable due to instability in rates. When the number of deaths is small, rankings by state may be unreliable due to instability in death rates.

[^cite_note-457]: &#x20;Kaiser Family Foundation (2022). ["Health Insurance Coverage of Children 0–18, 2019"](https://web.archive.org/web/20170509101229/https://www.kff.org/other/state-indicator/children-0-18/?currentTimeframe=0\&sortModel=%7B%22colId%22:%22Uninsured%22,%22sort%22:%22desc%22%7D). *kff.org*. San Francisco. Archived from [the original](https://www.kff.org/other/state-indicator/children-0-18/?currentTimeframe=0\&sortModel=%7B%22colId%22:%22Uninsured%22,%22sort%22:%22desc%22%7D) on May 9, 2017. Retrieved June 19, 2022. For more current data using the Current Population Survey, see Health Insurance Coverage of Children 0–18 (CPS). The majority of KFF health coverage topics are based on analysis of the Census Bureau's American Community Survey (ACS) by KFF. 2008–2019 American Community Survey, 1-Year Estimates includes a 1% sample of the US population and allows for precise state-level estimates. The ACS asks respondents about their health insurance coverage at the time of the survey. Respondents may report having more than one type of coverage; however, individuals are sorted into only one category of insurance coverage. See definitions on web page for more detail on coverage type.

[^cite_note-458]: &#x20;Kaiser Family Foundation (September 21, 2021). ["Health Insurance Coverage of the Total Population (CPS)"](https://www.kff.org/other/state-indicator/health-insurance-coverage-of-the-total-population-cps/?currentTimeframe=0\&selectedDistributions=uninsured\&sortModel=%7B%22colId%22:%22Uninsured%22,%22sort%22:%22desc%22%7D). *kff.org*. San Francisco. Retrieved June 19, 2022. For 2020, the majority of KFF health coverage topics are based on analysis of the Census Bureau's March Supplement to the Current Population Survey (the CPS Annual Social and Economic Supplement or ASEC). Previously, KFF source for these data was the Census Bureau's American Community Survey (ACS); however, release of the ACS data has been delayed due to the coronavirus pandemic. Although health coverage and population estimates using ACS are still available for 2008–2019, the 2020 data from CPS cannot be compared to prior year estimates from ACS. Due to known data quality issues with the 2019 CPS ASEC data, which was collected in March 2020 just at the onset of the pandemic and experienced low response rates, KFF have chosen not to report the 2019 data. KFF provide trend data for 2016, 2018, and 2020 using the CPS.
